## E7 · Discovery UI

- [x] **PG-E7-01** `M` · deps PG-E2-01, PG-E21-05 · App shell layout: sidebar, top bar, content area, persistent player surface
  - Acceptance: Shell never unmounts across navigation
  - Landed: `AppShell` is one column — the platform window chrome, a row holding `packages/ui`'s `Sidebar` beside the content area, and the player bar — so only `main`'s children change when the screen does. `apps/desktop/src/app/screens.ts` is the screen table (id, catalogue key, icon) and the shell draws one sidebar row per entry in that order, marking the current one with `aria-current="page"`; the core's `navigation.*` shortcuts route through the same table, so a shortcut cannot open a screen the sidebar does not list. `App.test.tsx` asserts the property the acceptance names — chrome, nav landmark, and player bar all render outside `<main>` — and that the row count and order follow the table rather than a second list. **The declared dep is only half delivered:** PG-E21-05's sidebar and its breakpoints are here; split panes, density modes, and user-driven collapse are not, so that task stays open. **Not this task's:** the screen switch is still `useState` (PG-E7-02 replaces it with typed routes, at which point a row becomes a link), and the player surface is the idle bar because the surface a playing session drives is PG-E16-03. Diagnostics stays a sidebar entry rather than moving under Settings, because `menu.rs` and the deep-link router both reach it directly today.
- [ ] **PG-E7-02** `M` · deps PG-E7-01, PG-E3-11 · Router with typed routes and cache-first loaders **The typed routes landed and its acceptance is tested; the loaders are the half that remains.** `apps/desktop/src/app/routes.ts` is the route space as a type — the four rows of `ARCHITECTURE` §6 this build has screens for — with `parseRoute` and `routePath` as the only readers and writers of a path, so a route the sidebar can open and a path a link can carry cannot disagree; the round trip is the test. **No router package was needed for this half, and that is a decision rather than a shortcut:** what such a package carries here is the cache-first loaders, which the screens' own hooks already do (a screen draws its own shape immediately and fills it when the read answers), while the route space itself is four paths, one optional parameter, and three consumers that must agree — the sidebar, the shortcut dispatcher, and the path the window opened at. `screens.ts` is now keyed by route rather than a second union of names, so the shortcut table and the sidebar are checked against each other by a test rather than by eye. **The acceptance — "no route renders a full-page spinner" — is a test over the table**, not over the route a default render happens to open: every sidebar entry renders its own heading in the content area with its navigation intact. The first version asserted "contains no progressbar", which was wrong for a reason worth recording: Diagnostics shows a progress meter *as* its content, and what the acceptance protects against is a page whose only content is a wait. **The path follows the route** (`lib/window-path.ts` replaces rather than pushes, because the history is the application's own — PG-E7-23 — and an OS back gesture should not walk sidebar clicks), so a reload returns to the screen the user was on. Verified in the running app: opening `/library` lands on Library, clicking Settings moves the window to `/settings` with the sidebar following. **Owed:** the cache-first loaders themselves, and the routes for screens that do not exist yet — `/browse`, `/search`, `/title/:kind/:id`, `/downloads`, `/player/:sessionId`, `/cast` — which land with their screens, the same rule the sidebar states. The `:section` and `:type` parameters are deliberately not modelled until something reads them. **`/browse/:type?` landed with the Browse screen (`PG-E7-09`), which is the second parameterised row after `/title` and the first whose optional half is a real default rather than an absent filter** — a provider's queried list is one kind, so a bare `/browse` means the default kind and the toolbar's switch writes the other one into the path. The cache-first loaders and the remaining rows (`/search`, `/downloads`, `/player/:sessionId`, `/cast`) are still owed.
- [x] **PG-E7-03** `M` · deps PG-E21-03 · Design-system primitives consumed from `packages/ui`
  - Acceptance: No ad-hoc styled element in a feature folder (review gate)
  - Landed across three slices, and the gate was audited rather than assumed — every `className` left in a feature folder is now one of two kinds: placement on a kit component (`m-auto max-w-md` on Discover's empty state, `flex flex-wrap gap-2` around the Diagnostics button row) or type and colour on the feature's own text. **The line, so the gate is checkable rather than a matter of taste:** a feature may place what it renders and may set type and colour on its own words; it may not build a control, a surface, a table, or a titled region, because those are the kit's. `AppShell` draws itself from `Sidebar`/`SidebarItem`/`IconButton`; `DiscoverScreen` is `Screen` + `EmptyState`; `SettingsScreen` is `Screen` + two `SettingsSection`s; `JobsScreen` is `Screen` + `Section`s + `Table` + `Progress` + `Panel` + the two state blocks. **This is a standing gate, not a one-off:** it holds for the three screens that exist, and each screen still to come (Browse, Detail, Library, Queue, Player, Cast — ARCHITECTURE §6's routing map) re-runs it as it lands and brings whatever primitive it needs with it. PG-E21-03 stays open because the primitive list is not finished; the gate does not require it to be, only that no feature re-implements one.
  - Acceptance: No ad-hoc styled element in a feature folder (review gate)
- [x] **PG-E7-04** `M` · deps PG-E7-03 · Title card component: poster, blurhash, rating badge, availability overlay
  - Acceptance: Renders correctly at 5 sizes without layout shift
  - **Landed as `packages/ui`'s `TitleCard`, and its acceptance is met structurally rather than by eye alone.** The tile is a fixed width from a five-step ladder (88/112/144/176/216, now recorded in DESIGN_SYSTEM §4) around an `aspect-[2/3]` box, so the rectangle a poster occupies is decided before any bytes arrive: a poster that loads late, one whose blob an eviction took, and one no provider ever filled all draw at the same size. The tests assert the ratio at **every** size, that the five widths are distinct, multiples of 8, and ascending, and that a poster's bytes failing (`posterVisible`, the one rule a static render cannot reach) falls back to the placeholder instead of leaving a hole. Verified by rendering the gallery section in both themes at 2× through the DevTools protocol.
  - **Two defects the render found, both invisible to a test written first.** The availability chip sized itself to its text, so on the 88 px tile the word ran past the poster's edge and was clipped mid-glyph by the frame's own overflow; the chip is now pinned to the tile's edges, truncates, and keeps the dot so the state is never colour alone. And an empty label drew a chip carrying nothing but that dot, which is the same §2.2 violation from the other direction: an empty word is now no word.
  - **Owed, and named here rather than implied by the tick.** The blurhash itself is PG-E6-11's — the card takes the colour it decodes to and draws a neutral surface without one, so nothing here invents a placeholder. The hover and focus metadata panel is PG-E7-05's, which is why the tile carries no visible title text: the name is in the accessibility tree (composed from the title, the caller's one-line meta, the rating's spoken form, and the chip's word) and the metadata layer arrives with the preview ladder. The `--radius-*` and `--dur-*` families §4 and §5 specify are still PG-E21-07's, so the tile consumes the radius utility that exists and will pick up the ladder when that task lands. The poster mark is one addition to the icon set (`star`), because a badge's number is not a score without it and §9 forbids a glyph standing in for an icon.

A grid therefore scaled its revealed tile by 30 %, and the first and last columns lost the left and right of the panel under the scroller's edge; a screenshot of Browse found it. The card now forwards the flag, Browse and Library pass `false`, and rails keep the default that reserves the room. Still owed on this task: the trailer peek toggle, and the dwell's own visual pass by eye.

- [x] **PG-E7-06** `L` · deps PG-E7-04 · Virtualized grid with fixed tile geometry
  - Acceptance: 5 000 items at ≥60 fps; blurhash fills first paint
  - **Landed as `packages/ui`'s `Grid`, and the acceptance was measured rather than asserted.** The tile geometry is fixed by §4, so which tiles are on screen is arithmetic and not a layout pass: `gridWindow` (the visible slice, its overscan, and where it sits) and `gridColumns` (how many fit across) are pure functions with every boundary case in their tests — the top, the bottom, a viewport taller than the content, a configuration of zero before the first measurement, and an offset left past the end by a list that shrank. **Measured in the browser over a full end-to-end scroll of 5 000 items** at 1440×900: 239 frames, p50 16.7 ms, p95 16.8 ms, worst 17.0 ms, and **not one frame over 20 ms**, with the DOM holding at most 30 tiles while the spacer carried the grid's full 150 120 px. The number is recorded here rather than enforced: it was taken in headless Chrome on one machine, and the frame-time gate that would make it a CI obligation is PG-E7-20's.
  - **A defect the measurement found, which no markup test could.** The first version's root was an `overflow-y-auto` box with no height constraint, and an unconstrained scroll container grows to its content: the "viewport" became the whole 180 000 px spacer and **all 5 000 tiles were rendered** — a virtualized grid that had quietly stopped virtualizing. The root now fills its parent (`h-full`) and the precondition is documented at the type, because the caller is who knows how much room a grid has. The gallery shows 15–30 nodes for 5 000 items now, which is the property that makes the frame rate possible.
  - **Owed by name.** The blurhash *value* is PG-E6-11's, exactly as on the card: the grid draws whatever tile it is given, and the tile already reserves its box and shows the placeholder from the first paint, so this half follows the moment that task generates one. Arrow-key navigation inside the grid and the "row 3 of 12, item 7 of 20" announcements of §10 are PG-E7-19's model, the pin that honours a density setting is PG-E7-18's, and the frame-time gate is PG-E7-20's.
- [x] **PG-E7-07** `M` · deps PG-E7-04 · Horizontal rails with keyboard traversal and edge affordances
  - Acceptance: Arrow keys traverse; no scroll jank on 500-item rails
  - **Landed as `packages/ui`'s `Rail`, which is why its acceptance is met by arithmetic rather than by a click.** The test environment is `node` with `react-dom/server` (PG-E21-03's named gap), so the two decisions a rail makes are exported as pure functions and driven directly: `nextRailIndex` answers where `←`/`→`/`Home`/`End` take the focus — clamping at both ends rather than wrapping, answering `null` for every key it does not own so Escape stays the overlay stack's, and bringing an out-of-range position back to something reachable, which is the filter-change-under-a-held-index case. `showsChevrons` decides the two edge states with a **1 px tolerance**, measured rather than imagined: at 2× a rail scrolled fully to its end reports a third of a pixel of remaining travel, and a strict comparison leaves the forward chevron lit on an end that cannot move.
  - **One tab stop for the whole rail, and this is the reason the API takes a render function.** §2.7 asks for `tabindex=0` on the container's active tile and `-1` on the rest, which a rail cannot do with `children`: it cannot reach into an element it did not construct. `renderItem(item, tabIndex)` hands the value to the caller's tile, so a rail of twelve posters is one `Tab` press instead of twelve. The scroll listener is passive and coalesced to one measurement per frame (§2.2), and the scroller reserves `py-2` because a 2 px focus ring on a two-axis-clipped scroller is cut off on the first and last tile.
  - **A defect the render found.** The first version carried `snap-x snap-start`; snapping moves the resting scroll offset off zero, `showsChevrons` read that as "there is somewhere to go back to", and the back chevron was lit on a rail already at its start. Snapping is PG-E21-10's ("scroll choreography: rail snapping") and is not this task's to guess at, so it is out — the class was both a rule this task does not own and the cause of the wrong affordance.
  - **Owed by name.** `PageUp`/`PageDown`, the geometrically-nearest-item move between rails, and Escape layering are PG-E7-19's keyboard model, and the edge scrims and snap choreography are PG-E21-10's; the chevrons are plain buttons over the tiles until then. The `empty` slot is what PG-E7-08's "each rail has a defined empty state" will fill. Verified by rendering the gallery section in both themes at 2×: the forward chevron alone at rest, both edges mid-scroll, and no tile's focus ring clipped.
- [ ] **PG-E7-08** `L` · deps PG-E7-07 · Discover screen: hero, continue watching, top 10, on the air, upcoming, top rated, recommendations **Remaining:** The Continue Watching rail has an empty state until PG-E18-04 supplies progress; that task integrates the rail after Discover exists.
  - **Landed (this round):** the mark now has a second hoisted group — Browse orders in-progress first, then completed, then the provider order (a failed job is not hoisted; its red mark is the signal)
  - **Global, not page-local:** `downloadsFirst` merges the queue rows ahead of the provider page, so a downloaded title the provider never sent still opens Browse at the top. A title both sources hold keeps the provider's richer row and is not drawn twice; the other kind and a failed job stay where they are. — and Discover draws a **Recent Downloads** rail above Continue Watching, in the same in-progress-then-completed order, hidden when the queue holds neither. `DownloadJobDto` gained the cached `poster` reference so the rail draws the same tile Browse does. The Continue Watching rail already existed and stays empty until playback records progress (PG-E18-04).
  - **Hero and preview reference correction:** the hero reserves its height in the scrolling screen, uses directional scrims and a trailing thumbnail strip; Discover uses larger posters, and previews enlarge both width and height by 30%, with metadata inside the enlarged card. Rail clip padding includes the enlarged poster and shadow and keyboard focus receives the same stacking priority as hover.
  - **The grey wall, and what it cost to fix (PG-E5-06's slice).** With a live key the screen drew seven rails of 117 titles and 93 placeholder tiles, because the job had an `ARTWORK_BUDGET` of 24 and needed a *details* request per title to learn a poster path. The port's candidate now carries the artwork and the score a list answer already gives, the budget is deleted rather than raised, and the rails fill in one run: seven list requests and about 117 image requests paced by the existing token bucket, and a warm visit costs none. **Both hazards that opened are closed.** A rail listing wrote through the storage layer's only writer of `metadata_state`, so every rail title was stamped `partial` and **`refresh_due` would not start a details refresh** for a title whose poster was cached. Migration 0014 gives `MetadataState` the `listed` value, `record_list_answer` writes it, and the rule now reads "has a *details* read filled this in" off the state rather than off `last_synced_at`, which a list answer also sets — so a rail title with its poster on disk is asked about once and gets the runtime, genre, and cast only a details read carries. The write is a floor and not an assignment (`update_title_metadata`'s `CASE`), because every rails run re-lists the titles it drew: a bare assignment would lower a filled title back to `listed` on the next fetch and send the screen asking its provider for details it had already given, once per visit, for ever. `title.tmdb_rating` is no longer write-only either: the manifest read that hydrates a launch reads it, with `tmdb_votes`, `overview`, the dates, and both artwork paths. **What the fix could not reach, and what now does:** a row already stamped `partial` by a pre-0014 list answer was indistinguishable from a details read and was never re-asked. A predicate does exist and migration 0019 applies it — only a details read writes any of the nine details-only columns, so a `partial` row with all nine empty is one no details read produced, and it goes back to `listed`. The rule and the season column that landed with it are recorded under `PG-E6-09`. `sync_search` still discards the artwork and score its candidates now carry (filed, R30).
  - **The hero and the rails landed; the rail set is not the whole of §6's map.** `/` now draws a hero (the first item of the first non-empty rail, with its name, year and a link to the title) and one `Rail` of `TitleCard`s per rail, each with its own `empty` slot and no spinner anywhere — the screen draws the rails' geometry and says what to do when a rail is empty (configure a provider key in Settings). `discover::home_rails` is **cache-first**: it reads the catalogue and never touches the network, and a Refresh affordance starts the rails job and follows it through the existing `job://state` mechanism. **Owed:** Continue Watching (PG-E18-04 owns progress), Top 10, and the "because you watched" rail (PG-E7-15), which are the rails §6 lists that this slice did not build. **Membership is persisted now (migration 0015)** — the follow-up this note used to name. `discover_rail` holds which titles each rail held and in what order, and membership only: everything a rail draws is on the `title` row the list answer wrote, and the cached blob's path is a `stat`, so a manifest that copied them would be a second copy of the catalogue free to drift from the first. `Runtime::start` hydrates the in-memory map from it after the database opens, so a launch draws the last fetch's rails and starts nothing — seven lists change on the provider's clock, not on the user's — and a read that finds nothing hydrated starts the fetch, which is `FLOWS F2`'s rule unchanged. **The enqueue no longer depends on the credential read having finished**, and that is the defect this round found by reading the launch: the read is blocking and runs off the startup path (a keychain can raise a prompt), so the screen's first read used to arrive while it was still in the air and be answered `NoProvider` — telling a user who *has* a key to go and set one up, and starting nothing, so the rails stayed empty until they pressed Refresh once the prompt was dismissed. `ProviderRegistry` now says whether the read has finished, a pending read enqueues the fetch and answers `Fetching`, the job waits for the read before deciding it has nothing to ask, and `NoProvider` is answered only by a read that has finished. No visual check of this screen has been performed — the tests are static renders, so the layout is unobserved by eye (the last three UI changes each found a defect that way). **And one budget breach this round found by reading the log rather than the code:** with a full set of rails in hand, `discover::home_rails` answers **76 356 bytes** against `AGENTS §6`'s 64 KB IPC budget, and the guard says so on every read (`an IPC response is over the payload budget; return identifiers instead`). The answer is the whole screen — seven rails of twenty items, each with a name, a year, a score, a synopsis, a vote count, two paths and a hover-preview's worth of prose — so the fix is the one the guard names: the rails return what identifies an item and a projection fills the rest, or the read becomes one rail at a time. Neither is guessed at here, because both change the screen's contract and the manifest that now hydrates it; the warn line is the record, and it is not silent.
  - Acceptance: Each rail has a defined empty state
- [ ] **PG-E7-09** `L` · deps PG-E7-06 · Filter surface: type, genre, year, rating, runtime, language, country, provider, status, availability, sort
  - **Type selector slice:** search overlays the hero; Movies / TV shows select a typed core parameter for rails and provider search. Search filters before the page limit under the existing account scope. `/?type=movie` and `/?type=show` preserve the selection on reload. Remaining: the other filter dimensions.
  - **The Browse slice landed, and it is the first four dimensions of this task's list.** `/browse/:type?` is a real route and a sidebar screen (`ARCHITECTURE` §6's row), and it is the queried grid a rail cannot be: a rail takes no arguments, so "documentary films rated eight or better, newest first" was a question the port could not ask before this. The pieces, in the order a request travels: the port grew `browse(&BrowseRequest) -> ProviderPage` — a kind, a page, an order, a provider genre, and a floor under the score — and `genres(kind)`, because a provider's film and series vocabularies are separate lists with different numbers; TMDB answers them with `/discover/movie|tv` and `/genre/movie|tv/list`, and the order mapping is total in both order and kind because the two do not share a date field (`primary_release_date` against `first_air_date`). **Sorting by score carries a vote floor** (`RATING_VOTE_FLOOR`): TMDB orders by the raw average, so without one the first page is a wall of `10.0` from a single vote. `Runtime::browse` writes the page with `peregrine_sync::upsert_list_candidate` — the same writer the rails job uses, extracted so one film cannot be two titles on one screen and one on another — then fetches the page's posters concurrently, keyed so a second visit costs a `stat` per tile and no request. `Runtime::genres` caches per kind for the session, because a vocabulary changes on the provider's clock rather than the user's. **The frontend is `features/browse`**: a toolbar of kind, genre, order, and floor, over `packages/ui`'s virtualized `Grid` — the first screen to use it — with `onEndReached` on the grid driving the next page. Two rules are pure functions with their own tests because this repository's harness has no DOM: `gridEndReached` (never fires on an empty list, fires within the threshold and not before) and `browse-pages`'s `mergePage`/`mayLoad`. `mergePage` de-duplicates by catalogue row, because `sort_by=popularity.desc` is ordered by a number that moves while a reader scrolls, so a title really can appear on two pages — and the grid keys its tiles by that row, which would make a repeat a duplicate React key. **What is owed here and named rather than implied:** the filter set is URL-borne only for the kind (`/browse/movie`); genre, floor, and order live in the screen's state, so a filtered view is not yet shareable — which is this task's own acceptance, "filter state is URL-encoded and shareable", and it is the next slice. Year, runtime, language, country, provider, status, and availability are untouched, and so is `PG-E7-10`'s saved smart collection. **The search field is a search of everything, not a narrowing of the page.** A browse page has no text parameter, so the first version filtered the pages already received — and "Spiderman" matched nothing because the catalogue's index tokenises "Spider-Man" into two terms, a defect a screenshot found. The field now asks the providers (`useTitleSearch(kind, { tiles: true })`, the hook's tile mode over the shared debounce) and draws the matches as **poster tiles** in the same grid geometry as the page. `Runtime::search_tiles` — the tile-shaped sibling of `discover`, and a new IPC command — writes each provider candidate through `upsert_list_candidate`, the list writer a browse page already uses, then runs the same poster pass, so a result carries the year, the score, the synopsis, and the cached artwork path a `TitleCard` draws. It falls back to the local index when no provider is configured, or when the provider's answer is empty or fails — a provider having a bad minute must not blank a catalogue that holds the title. That fallback now folds the punctuation out of both sides (`spiderman` matches `Spider-Man`), is capped at 256 folded characters, and joins back to the index so an unindexed title stays unsearchable. It is a scan on an index miss, not a ranking path; `PG-E8-04` remains the normalized/trigram index. `Runtime::discover` also answers with the provider's own candidates read back as catalogue rows rather than re-searching text the index cannot match. The field is not URL-borne yet, like the rest of the filter row.
  - Acceptance: Filter state is URL-encoded and shareable
- [ ] **PG-E7-10** `M` · deps PG-E7-09, PG-E3-07 · Smart collections: saved filter plus sort, pinned to the sidebar
  - Acceptance: Persisted, reorderable, applied in one click
- [ ] **PG-E7-11** `L` · deps PG-E7-03, PG-E21-09 · Title detail: hero expansion, backdrop cross-fade, actions, availability **The route and the library-backed half landed.** `/title/:kind/:id` is the map's first parameterised route, and `apps/desktop/src/app/routes.ts` parses it with the kind checked against the two the core names: a path claiming a kind this build does not have is a path it does not open, and the id is refused unless it is a positive safe integer. **The kind is in the path because it is what a screen can draw before its read answers** — the page says Film or Series immediately, which is `PG-E7-02`'s acceptance applied to the one route that has a parameter. The screen shows the library's own answer: the title's totals, and a series' episodes in the order they are watched, with an episode nobody has named yet saying so rather than showing an empty cell. `null` from `get_title` covers *both* "this account may not see it" and "no title has that id" (I37), and the page says the title is not here for either rather than reporting a failure — a `missing` state, not an error state, with a test for each. Titles in the library list are buttons that open the page. **The poster landed, over the asset protocol rather than IPC.** `get_title` answers a `TitleDetailDto` — the library's own answer, plus `poster`, an absolute path in the image cache, plus `refreshDue` — and `TitleScreen` draws it beside the totals at the poster's own 2:3 ratio, with the placeholder in its place when the cache holds none. The path is a path and never bytes (`AGENTS R11`), `lib/media.ts` is still the only module that names `convertFileSrc`, and a reference that is not a cache path renders the placeholder rather than an `<img>` the CSP would refuse. **The fetch has a caller for the first time:** `refreshDue` is the core's rule, not the screen's (`crates/peregrine-app/src/artwork.rs`), and `useTitle` asks for one refresh per visit when it is true — so opening the same title twice waits on one job (`PG-E6-09`'s dedupe key) and a title whose provider has no artwork is never asked about again. **Owed, and most of what this task is:** the hero expansion and the backdrop cross-fade (the backdrop is carried now but nothing fetches one for a title whose provider gave none), cast, and what the actions *do* once they can act — `PG-E5`, `PG-E6`, `PG-E13-15` for acquisition's label and behaviour, and `PG-E16` for the player — plus a link primitive rather than a button, which is `PG-E21-03`'s list. **The availability half landed, and with it the rule that a title the library holds is never offered for download.** `peregrine_domain::TitleOffer` is four values derived from what SM2 says about the title's items — `play`, `arriving`, `absent`, `acquire` — and it exists as a rule rather than as a boolean a screen could compute from a count, because a count cannot tell a copy on disk from one the library remembers and cannot see. The read that decides is `peregrine-storage`'s `title_evidence`: three statements returning every item with its own files and jobs, plus the title's whole job set; `Runtime::library_title` feeds the first through `availability` per item and the second through `TitleOffer::of`. **The job set is the title's rather than the items' on purpose:** `download_job.media_item_id` is nullable because acquisition is requested before the import creates the item (`PG-E14-19`), so a rule reading only the items would answer `acquire` for a title whose transfer is running — the same defect from the other side. **The wire gained one field** (`TitleDetailDto.offer`, generated bindings included) and `lib/play-action.ts` gained `titlePageActions` beside `heroPlay`: Discover's rail keeps its boolean because a rail of twenty tiles is not worth the whole derivation, while the title page draws Download only for `acquire` — absent rather than disabled, since a disabled Download beside a working Play still reads as an offer to fetch what the user has — and says the core's own sentence for `absent` ("recorded and not on disk") as against `acquire` ("holds no file yet"), two facts a count had been collapsing into one. **Deliberately not here:** the title-scoped badge reduction the watchlist needs is `PG-E9-05`'s; `Asked::Nothing` is passed and is exact, because nothing in this build writes `watchlist_entry` and no command requests an acquisition, so nothing can have asked for anything yet; and the acquisition control still fires nothing, because `PG-E13` owns the queue it would start — `PG-E13-15` is where "one primary action per surface, whose label and behaviour follow the job's state" is written, and it is that task that decides what this control is called once it can act.
  - **The recommendations rail landed, which is the part of this task `PG-E7-15` owns and the page draws.** A title's page shows "More like this" under its episodes, from the provider's own answer — read by the core, which is where the two facts belong: the identifier asked for is the one *that* provider owns (a title may carry several providers' ids, and asking one of them for another's number is how a film gets another film's recommendations), and a title no configured provider holds an id for is answered empty rather than as a failure, so the page draws no rail for it rather than an error. **The rail's order is the provider adapter's, and it leads with the film's collection** (`PG-E7-15` records the *Avatar* defect that made that necessary); the rail draws what it is handed, is one page, and draws nothing when it is empty. **It also draws its heading once**: a `Section` wrapped around a `Rail` printed "More like this" twice, which a screenshot of the running app showed and a test now pins. **Still owed from this task's own list:** the hero expansion and backdrop cross-fade, cast, and what the actions do once they can act — the availability those actions are drawn from landed with the note above.
  - **Owed, and asked for by a reader: genre shelves under the recommendations, each endlessly scrollable.** "Spider-Man: Brand New Day" should offer "More Action", "More Adventure" and so on beneath the rail, each a paged grid rather than a strip. **The paging half already exists** — `discover::browse` takes a kind, a genre, a page, an order and a score floor, and `packages/ui`'s `Grid` with `onEndReached` and the reserved placeholder row is the endless surface `PG-E7-09` built — so the shelf is a composition of two things that work, plus one thing that does not exist: **the seed title's own genres**. TMDB's `/movie|tv/{id}` answers `genres: [{id, name}]`, and this build reads none of it: `DetailsAnswer` does not deserialize the field, `TitleDetails` has no place to put it, nothing writes `title_genre` (the table holds zero rows in a live database), and the port has no way to ask "which genres is this title". So the slice is: read `genres` in the adapter, carry it to the runtime, and let the title page render one shelf per genre id through the browse read. It is **not** implemented here, and it is not a screen-only change — the first honest step is the domain/provider field, which is the same prerequisite `docs/research/recommendations.md` names as S1/S3.
  - **Owed, and fixed at the container rather than the symptom: nothing in this shell may pan sideways.** A reader reported the whole view gaining a horizontal scrollbar, with the content sliding under the sidebar. The mechanism was measured, not guessed: `overflow-y-auto` computes `overflow-x` to `auto`, the rail's root leaked its deliberate overhang (+67 px) with `overflow: visible`, and the page absorbed it — so the *page* scrolled horizontally and its own heading slid off. Three containers now refuse it: `Screen` is `overflow-x-hidden` with `min-w-0`, `Grid` is `overflow-x-hidden` (its column count is derived to fit, so anything left over is a measurement race and must be clipped), and `Rail` is `overflow-x-clip` so it contains its own overhang without becoming a second scroll container. Verified by rendering the real frontend under a mocked bridge and measuring `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at 1440 and 1100 on both `/browse` and a title page, with the rail's strip the only remaining horizontal scroller. **The lesson worth keeping:** the previous three UI changes were never looked at by eye, and all three defects this round were visible in a screenshot — a scripted render-plus-measure harness belongs in the repository, and no task owns one yet.
  - Acceptance: Shared-element transition from card; reduced-motion path exists
- [ ] **PG-E7-12** `M` · deps PG-E7-11 · Cast and crew presentation with person pages
  - Acceptance: Person page lists credits and is reachable by keyboard
- [ ] **PG-E7-13** `L` · deps PG-E7-11, PG-E4-10 · Season selector and episode list with stills, air dates, progress **The data this screen reads now exists**: `refresh_title` fills a series' seasons and their episodes from TMDB and the job caches the posters and stills (`PG-E5-06`'s season slice), so the names, air dates, runtimes, stills, and scores are stored rather than only read. **Still owed here:** the selector's and list's own interaction work, specials marking, missing-episode indication, and the progress column. **One shape this screen used to draw, and no longer can:** a first visit to a show nobody had filled in read an empty season list, started the refresh, and never read again — so the page stayed empty until the reader left and came back. `useSeasons` now takes the page's own refresh count and re-runs both reads when it moves, and the season read follows the `kind` the title *answered* rather than the one the route spelled, because the player's way back and a download row can both spell "movie" for a show. The catalogue half of that state — why some shows had no seasons to read at all — is recorded under `PG-E6-09`'s rule.
  - Acceptance: Specials marked; missing episodes indicated
- [ ] **PG-E7-14** `M` · deps PG-E7-11, PG-E24-06 · Trailer playback in a sandboxed child webview with privacy-enhanced embed
  - Acceptance: No third-party script loads in the main window (asserted)
- [ ] **PG-E7-15** `M` · deps PG-E7-08, PG-E18-04 · Recommendations and "because you watched" based on local history
  - **The provider's half landed and was then rewritten around the reported bug; the local-ranking half is the whole of what remains.** A title's own page draws a "More like this" rail under its episodes, read from `discover::recommendations` via the port's `similar(id, kind)`. **The reported defect was that opening *Avatar: Fire and Ash* showed A.I., Evil Dead, Close Encounters and a wall of Harry Potter**, and the research that settled it is [research/recommendations.md](./research/recommendations.md) — which records that TMDB's own staff call `/similar` "just an aggregation around genres and keywords" over user-contributed data, "does not tend to yield very good results", and urge `/recommendations` instead. The adapter now answers from `/recommendations`, with `/similar` kept as the fallback for the case those same staff name — "still not perfect, especially for new releases", which is exactly what *Fire and Ash* is — and it leads with the film's **collection**, which is TMDB's one exact franchise signal (`belongs_to_collection` → `/collection/{id}`), ordered by release date and with the opened film excluded. A series asks for no collection at all, because TMDB models franchises for films only. Three facts are the core's rather than a screen's, and each is tested where it belongs: the identifier asked for is the one *that* provider owns (asking one provider for another's number is how a film gets another film's recommendations, the rule `peregrine_sync::refresh_title` states from the other direction); a title no configured provider holds an id for is answered **empty rather than as a failure**, so the page draws no rail instead of an error beside the episodes; and the collection lookup is **best-effort**, because a rail whose franchise request failed should be the provider's list rather than a failed page. **What is owed, and the research doc is the plan:** every slice from S1 on — a `collection` table so the franchise tier stops costing a request, the reverse indexes on `title_genre`/`title_keyword` that a local candidate query needs, and the whole local content-similarity ranking (IDF-weighted genre and keyword overlap, BM25 over the overview, era, runtime, language, people, a Bayesian tiebreak, tiers, and typed reasons). `Runtime::similar` today returns the provider's list unchanged: **no local ranking, no tiers, no reasons**, so this task's acceptance is not met and it stays open. Also owed, and separate: "because you watched" from `PG-E18-04`'s progress, which the research doc argues should be its **own** rail rather than a silent change of meaning in this one.
  - Acceptance: Requires no server; degrades to trending when history is empty
- [ ] **PG-E7-16** `M` · deps PG-E7-08 · Empty, loading, and error states for every rail and grid **Remaining:** The three screens that exist have designed states rather than spinners — Discover's whole body is `EmptyState` at page size, Diagnostics draws `EmptyState` per region, Settings distinguishes an empty registry from an unreadable one — but every one of those is a *single* state, and this task is about the state matrix each rail and grid will have once they carry data: cached-then-revalidating, offline, partial, failed, and genuinely empty. **Blocked on the data path, not on design:** the rails are PG-E7-08's and arrive with the provider cache (PG-E5, PG-E6). Skeletons come from PG-E21-12.
  - **The paging states landed with Browse, and they are the first of this task's matrix that had a real caller.** `Grid` renders a reserved row of placeholder tiles *inside its own flow* while `loading` is set, at the caller's tile geometry, and carries that reservation in its spacer height — so the scrollbar is honest about the page on its way and the arrival of the real tiles moves nothing. The reserved cells are `aria-hidden`, because a skeleton is decoration and the fact that something is loading belongs in words: the Browse screen's footer says either "Loading more…" or "That is everything for these filters.", the second being the state a reader needs most and the one a scroll with no end-marker cannot express. Both are `role="status"`, so the change is announced rather than only drawn. What is still owed here is the rest of the matrix — cached-then-revalidating, offline, partial, and failed as a *per-rail* treatment rather than the page-level `ErrorState` that exists — and the skeletons `PG-E21-12` owes for the *first* paint, which is a different reservation from this one.
  - Acceptance: Every state has a designed treatment, not a spinner

The window's path stays `replaceState`: the history is the application's, so an OS back gesture cannot walk sidebar clicks.

# Phase 2 — Acquisition and library

## E10 · Torrent engine

- [x] **PG-E10-01** `M` · deps PG-E4-19 · `TorrentEngine` trait: session, add, select, stream, stats, limits, remove
  - Acceptance: Fake engine passes the conformance suite
  - Done: the port is `crates/peregrine-torrent/src/engine.rs` — descriptor, `add`, `select`, `stream`, `stats`, `state`, `verify`, `limits`/`set_limits`, `pause`, `resume`, `remove`, `shutdown` — object-safe through `EngineFuture`, a boxed `Send` future on the `ProviderFuture` model, with `Arc<dyn TorrentEngine>` and an await through the trait both tested. **The gate is a type, not a rule:** `AddRequest::new` is the only constructor, takes the `SafetyReport`, and returns `AddRefused::{OptInRequired, Blocked}` unless the verdict is `Verdict::Allowed`, so "add this anyway" has no spelling — and it holds for the recovery intent too, because a reseed writes into the same directory a first download would (SECURITY T12, FLOWS F6). The destination is a `SafePath`, so the engine cannot receive a path that did not come through `PathGuard` (ADR 0005). `InfoHash`, `TorrentSource`, `EngineState`, and `AddRequest` have hand-written redacting `Debug`s and no `Display`, and every `EngineError` variant renders a fixed sentence with no interpolated value — driven through `peregrine_domain::check_text` with a real path, a magnet, and a 40-hex canary. No third-party type appears in a public signature and `librqbit` is deliberately absent until PG-E10-02. **The fake and the suite are the acceptance:** `FakeTorrentEngine` is in `peregrine-testkit/src/torrent.rs`, `torrent_engine_satisfies_the_port` in `conformance.rs` passes against it and fails against a wrapper that misreports `stats` for another info hash, so the suite is shown non-vacuous. Decisions the port had to fix because nothing else could: an empty selection is representable and refused by `add` and `select` (`EmptySelection`); `remove` is idempotent, and after `shutdown` every operation but `shutdown` is `SessionClosed`; `stream` at the file's length is a legitimate empty range and past it is `OffsetPastEnd`; `pause` and `resume` are levels, so refusing a duplicate event stays in `download::transition`. **Owed, and recorded on the tasks that own it rather than here:** the metadata-only fetch this port has no operation for (PG-E10-03 — FLOWS F6 cannot be spelled without it, because `add` selects indices into a file list only that fetch produces), the DHT, tracker, and port detail ARCHITECTURE §15 promises a stalled user (PG-E10-07), and whether "active downloads" is an engine limit at all (PG-E10-10). The recovery surface extends what ADR 0005's decision listed; the ADR now records it.
- [ ] **PG-E10-02** `L` · deps PG-E10-01 · librqbit adapter: session lifecycle, config, persistence path
  - Acceptance: Start/stop clean on all three OSes; SPIKE-3 outcome applied
- [ ] **PG-E10-03** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Metadata-only fetch for magnets with timeout and peer reporting
  - Acceptance: Returns the file list without writing payload bytes
  - Required by PG-E10-01: this operation belongs on the `TorrentEngine` trait rather than beside it. `AddRequest` selects file indices into a metadata file list, and this is the only operation that produces one, so a service cannot spell FLOWS F6 — metadata, then inspection, then `add` — without it. It also has to name the value the list arrives in, and the port does not yet have that type: the domain's `TorrentDescription`/`TorrentEntry` is what the inspection gate already reads paths, sizes, and entry kinds from, so the metadata fetch should produce that rather than a second file-list vocabulary.
- [ ] **PG-E10-04** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Per-file selection and deselection at add time and afterwards
  - Acceptance: Unselected files never appear on disk
- [ ] **PG-E10-05** `L` · deps PG-E10-02 · Per-job "download in order" toggle plus head-and-tail-first ordering and streaming read-ahead with a configurable, bitrate-derived window
  - Acceptance: First playable byte within the target time on a local swarm; the container index is present from the start so seeking works immediately; toggling off restores rarest-first outside the window
- [ ] **PG-E10-06** `M` · deps PG-E10-05, PG-E16-06 · Piece priority driver reacting to player read position and stalls
  - Acceptance: Starvation raises priority within one poll interval
- [ ] **PG-E10-07** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Progress and statistics stream: rates, peers, ETA, ratio, health
  - Acceptance: Stats accurate within 5% of engine counters
  - Required by PG-E10-01: `TorrentStats` carries downloaded, uploaded, total, rates, peer counts, ETA, ratio, and health, and nothing else. ARCHITECTURE §15's "Torrent stalled" row promises the actionable detail behind a stall — DHT nodes, tracker status, port reachability — so this task decides deliberately whether that is more fields on the port's statistics or a separate call, rather than leaving PG-E13-13's panel to invent a source. Port reachability itself is PG-E10-13's.
  - **The poll side landed, and it was the missing half of the queue screen.** `AcquisitionService::statistics` writes one coalesced tick and publishes `download://stats`, but it had no non-test caller: a real transfer moved bytes while `download_job` stayed at zero and no event fired, so the Downloads screen drew `downloading` at 0 % with 0/0 peers for a transfer that was actually running and a Browse tile never learned a download had started. `AcquisitionService::start_pump` now spawns a one-second task whose `sample` asks every job this session holds for a tick — ids taken under the handle lock and the lock released before any await, and a refused tick logged and skipped so one finished job cannot stop the rest — and `Runtime::start` starts it once a real engine exists while `Runtime::shutdown` stops it before the bus. Still owed: the accuracy acceptance (no engine-counter comparison yet), the stalled detail §15 promises, and a rule for a seeding job, whose `Progress` the domain currently refuses.
- [ ] **PG-E10-08** `L` · deps PG-E10-02, PG-E3-08 · Restart reattachment from `engine_state_json`
  - Acceptance: Kill and relaunch resumes without re-downloading verified pieces
  - Required: recovery is the application's job, never the user's. An interrupted download repairs itself in the background — re-attaching the payload, verifying pieces rather than fetching them again, and re-attaching a completed payload to seed it again ("force reseeding") — with no repair step offered to the user at all. The schema already carries what this needs (`magnet_uri`, `torrent_file_path`, and `engine_state_json` in DATA_MODEL §2.7), so no migration is implied. The `.torrent`'s **contents** cross the engine port rather than `torrent_file_path` itself: that file lives in the application's own store, and ADR 0005's rule is that the engine never receives a path outside the download root.
- [ ] **PG-E10-09** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Seeding policy: ratio target, time target, stop-on-quit, keep-seeding
  - Acceptance: Terminates at the configured policy; survives restart
  - Required: "force reseeding" is a policy this task owns — when a completed payload is re-attached to seed, and for how long — while the operation it calls belongs to PG-E10-08. A re-attach runs the gate again on the stored metainfo, because D5 in SECURITY.md and I4 in DATA_MODEL let a job reach `metadata` only through a report it holds: re-adding is not a way to skip inspection.
- [ ] **PG-E10-10** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Global and per-job limits: down, up, connections, active downloads
  - Acceptance: Applied at runtime without restarting the session
  - Open by PG-E10-01: `EngineLimits` carries `active_downloads` because this task names it, but no document says which layer owns the value, and the two candidates differ in kind — the engine would enforce it by holding torrents back, while the service would enforce it by admitting fewer jobs, which is what PG-E6-01's priority queues and worker pool already exist to do. Settle it here, write the answer into ARCHITECTURE §5 under `peregrine-torrent` or the scheduler, and drop the field from the port if the service owns it: a knob the engine is handed and does not own is a second place for the active set to live.
- [ ] **PG-E10-11** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Proxy and interface binding: `socks5h://` (so DNS resolves at the proxy), interface bind, explicit handling of DHT and UDP-tracker traffic while a proxy is set, **runtime re-resolution** of the bound interface, and **fail-closed** behaviour when it disappears
  - Acceptance: Engine peer and tracker IO stops entirely if the bound interface goes away, no silent fallback to the default route, and a VPN reconnect is picked up without an app restart (qBittorrent passes the unresolved interface through but never re-resolves it, so a dropped tunnel just grinds); asserted by killing and restoring the interface in a test
- [ ] **PG-E10-12** `M` · deps PG-E10-11 · Leak check tool: compare the tunnel-exit IP with the machine's non-tunnelled public IP via several echo endpoints
  - Acceptance: Reports the observed addresses and states plainly which protocols were verified; SOCKS5 UDP-associate coverage is explicitly reported as unverified until SPIKE-3 confirms it
- [ ] **PG-E10-13** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Port reachability check and UPnP/NAT-PMP decision, off by default
  - Acceptance: Diagnostics shows reachability; no silent router mutation
- [ ] **PG-E10-14** `M` · deps PG-E10-02 · Loopback seeder harness for integration tests
  - Acceptance: A test downloads from a local seeder deterministically
- [ ] **PG-E10-15** `S` · deps PG-E10-02 · Engine error taxonomy mapped to UI codes
  - Acceptance: No raw engine string reaches the UI
- [ ] **PG-E10-16** `M` · deps PG-E10-10, PG-E16-01 · Bandwidth reservation while playing and "pause on playback" policy
  - Acceptance: Streaming keeps headroom; verified under a saturating download
- [ ] **PG-E10-17** `M` · deps PG-E10-14 · RSS ingest adapter (Phase 5 prerequisite, engine side)
  - Acceptance: Parses a fixture feed and enqueues candidates
- [ ] **PG-E10-18** `M` · deps PG-E10-11 · IP-filter and blocklist support with **fail-closed** parse semantics: an unparseable filter blocks new peer traffic with a prominent error and requires an explicit "continue without blocklist" confirmation, never silently installing an empty blocklist. Plain-text and gzip formats, applied on file change without a restart
  - Acceptance: A corrupt filter file blocks new peer traffic and surfaces an actionable error (qBittorrent's parser installs an empty filter, which is fail-open); a valid edit applies live; both formats covered by fixtures
- [x] **PG-E10-19** `L` · deps PG-E10-01, PG-E3-08 · `AcquisitionService`: the F6 write path — inspect, persist, hand to the engine, follow it, complete
  - Acceptance: A decided release becomes a `download_job` row whose every state change came out of `download::transition`, an `AddRequest` built from a report persisted before the add, and a job followed through its statistics that can be paused, resumed, and removed — driven end to end against `FakeTorrentEngine` with no network
  - **Completion landed (this round):** `AcquisitionService::statistics` now applies `PayloadComplete` then `SeedingSatisfied` when a tick reports the whole payload, so a finished transfer reaches `completed` instead of staying `downloading` at 100 % for ever. `finish_download` drops the session handle before the transitions, imports what the payload wrote, and stops the torrent (`EngineError`-free removal keeps the files); `reconcile_finished` finishes the jobs a previous process left at a whole payload, called from the composition root before the pump starts. Tests: `a_complete_payload_is_imported_and_can_be_deleted` (also the import and deletion below), plus the new `with_downloaded` builder on `FakeTorrentEngine`. Seeding policy (PG-E10-09) and reattach (PG-E10-08) are still owed: a payload stops seeding immediately and a job killed mid-transfer is not reattached.
  - Done: `crates/peregrine-app/src/acquisition.rs` — `start`, `consent`, `pause`, `resume`, `remove`, and `statistics`, over a `FreeSpace` port so admission is testable without a disk. **The pre-write guarantee is control flow, not a comment:** `start` resolves the title through the scoped identity read (an out-of-scope title and a missing one are the same refusal, R34), resolves the destination through `PathGuard`, inserts the row at `queued`, applies `Admitted` then `Inspected(report)` so the report is persisted **before** anything reaches the engine, and returns the parked row with its verdict when the report is not `Verdict::Allowed` — `hand_off` is never called. `a_blocked_or_opt_in_torrent_never_reaches_the_engine` asserts that through the fake's call log rather than by inspection. Admission's free-space floor runs before the add for a job the gate allowed, so one that cannot fit stays `queued` with nothing to undo, while one the gate refused still reports the refusal (see the review note below); `statistics` re-checks the floor on every accepted tick and pauses an active transfer through `DiskFull` rather than failing it (ARCHITECTURE §15). The add is built from the report the transition persisted, and the engine's own state is recorded after acceptance, so a restart has it. Statistics are coalesced by a per-job `written_at` against the injected clock and publish one event on the download topic, so the whole path is deterministic under `FakeClock`. A blocked row can explain itself: `DownloadJobRow.safety_report` deserializes the persisted JSON back into the domain `SafetyReport`, which is what PG-E11-07 renders. **Not assembled anywhere yet, deliberately:** `Runtime` cannot construct the service until an engine exists (PG-E10-02), and no command or screen calls it — the state PG-E1-19 recorded for its own service. `can_acquire` and rating ceilings are PG-E35-01/PG-E35-05's and are not consulted here. **Review:** the first draft's `GateRefused` error variant was removed — a refused gate hands back the parked row and its verdict, so an error for it had no caller (R3).
  - The service is `peregrine-app`'s, per ARCHITECTURE §5's service list, and it is the only place the inspection gate, the engine port, the job journal, and the download repositories are combined.
  - **Split deliberately at the gate.** The metadata-only fetch F6 needs before inspection is PG-E10-03's, so this task takes a `TorrentDescription` the caller already has — which is precisely what `TorrentInspector::inspect` consumes — and PG-E10-03 becomes the adapter that produces one from a magnet or a `.torrent` file. That keeps the slice offline and testable end to end, and leaves PG-E10-03 swapping a supplier rather than restructuring a service.
  - **Review, after the gates:** the consent path had to be completed. The first draft advanced a consented job to `metadata` and stopped, on the reading that the metadata fetch picks it up later — but F6's two branches merge back into the same `add` step, the user's confirmation being the last thing before the transfer starts, and `download.rs` defines `metadata` as "the gate allowed the torrent and it is **being handed to the engine**" with a timeout as its only other exit. A job parked there is a download that never begins, and the description needed to add it was already in hand at the moment of consent. `consent` now checks admission, lifts the block, and completes the handoff through the same private `hand_off` that a first add uses. Admission is checked **before** the block is lifted, so a full volume leaves the job `blocked` — where the same consent can be given again once there is room — rather than in `metadata`. The free-space check in `start` also refuses only a job the gate allowed: a blocked torrent reports the block even on a full volume, because the verdict was already computed and answering "free some space" sends the user to do that for a download that still cannot start. Three tests hold both: `consent_lifts_an_opt_in_block_and_completes_the_add`, `consent_on_a_full_volume_leaves_the_job_blocked`, and `a_refused_torrent_says_so_even_when_the_volume_is_full`.
  - Its storage half is PG-E3-10's `download_job` and `download_file` repositories, which land first: the service cannot be written without them, and their acceptance is the round-trip property test that task asks for.

## E11 · Safety gate
